Um, outside the office? No, definitely not. I'm completely relaxed when I'm not at work. But... Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to make a big deal out of it, because offices are naturally kind of noisy, right? But... yeah, I suppose that's actually the main reason I ended up calling the Employee Assistance Program. 

It's just... lately, every little bang in the office—like, if someone slams a file drawer shut too hard, or drops a coffee mug in the breakroom—I just jump really hard. Like, way more than I should. And then my chest just keeps racing for a long time afterwards. It's silly, I know. I really try to play it off so the guys don't notice, because a supervisor shouldn't be jumping at shadows, right? I just... I want to be strong for my team. Is it normal to get a little jumpy like that when you're under stress? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting by bringing it up.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm *constantly* on guard. That sounds a little extreme, you know? But... I guess I have been a little more aware of things lately. 

It's just, when I'm at my desk, I find myself kind of... checking where my boss is, or making sure I know where the doors are. And if someone walks up behind me, I kind of need to know they're there. I mean, I'm supposed to be focused on my team and helping them out, but I just keep glancing around. I try not to make it obvious, because I don't want the guys thinking I'm distracted or, you know, paranoid or something. It's probably just me being a little too cautious, right? I mean, it's good to be aware of your surroundings, isn't it? I just hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about these reactions happening again, even when you're not in the office or hearing loud noises?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Once I leave the office and I'm just at home or out, I'm actually... I'm pretty okay. I don't sit around stressing about whether I'm going to jump at a noise tomorrow, or anything like that. \n\nIt's really just in the moment, when the loud noise actually happens. I try to leave work at work, you know? I hope that's a good sign? Like I said, I really don't want to make it sound like this is ruining my whole life or anything, because I'm totally fine most of the time. Does that make sense for what you're asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding any specific places, situations, or activities because you're worried about being startled or having that racing chest feeling?\n\nPatient: Um, no, definitely not. I still do everything I normally do. I mean, I still go into the office every day to work with my sales team—I wouldn't want to let them down or call out just because I'm a little jumpy sometimes. \n\nI just try to push through it, you know? I'm not hiding out at home or avoiding any places or activities at all. I figure it's better to just keep my normal routine and not make a big deal out of it, right? I really hope that's the right way to handle things. I'm just trying to keep everything as normal as possible... did that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Even though you don't avoid places or activities, do you ever find yourself worrying in the back of your mind that you might get startled again in the future?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, maybe there's a tiny, fleeting thought sometimes if I see someone drop something heavy, but it's not like a constant worry or anything that bothers me. I just brush it off. I just don't want to seem like I'm constantly complaining or worrying over nothing, you know?\n\nDoctor: Outside of the office, do you generally feel like you're more on edge or alert than most people, even in calm or safe environments?\n\nPatient: Um, outside the office? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, I wouldn't say I get upset. I mean, it's a sales floor, so interruptions are just part of the job, right? You kind of have to roll with it. 

If someone comes up to me with a question, I'm always happy to help them out. That's what I'm there for as a supervisor. I guess... maybe sometimes I feel a tiny bit rushed if I'm right in the middle of something, but I never snap at anyone or get angry. I try really hard to keep a positive attitude for the team. Being approachable is super important to me, so I definitely don't let little things get to me. I think I handle the day-to-day stress pretty well, don't you think? It's just normal work stuff.

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about these reactions happening again, even when you're not in the office or hearing loud noises?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Once I leave the office and I'm just at home or out, I'm actually... I'm pretty okay. I don't sit around stressing about whether I'm going to jump at a noise tomorrow, or anything like that. \n\nIt's really just in the moment, when the loud noise actually happens. I try to leave work at work, you know? I hope that's a good sign? Like I said, I really don't want to make it sound like this is ruining my whole life or anything, because I'm totally fine most of the time. Does that make sense for what you're asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding any specific places, situations, or activities because you're worried about being startled or having that racing chest feeling?\n\nPatient: Um, no, definitely not. I still do everything I normally do. I mean, I still go into the office every day to work with my sales team—I wouldn't want to let them down or call out just because I'm a little jumpy sometimes. \n\nI just try to push through it, you know? I'm not hiding out at home or avoiding any places or activities at all. I figure it's better to just keep my normal routine and not make a big deal out of it, right? I really hope that's the right way to handle things. I'm just trying to keep everything as normal as possible... did that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Even though you don't avoid places or activities, do you ever find yourself worrying in the back of your mind that you might get startled again in the future?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't sit around dwelling on it or anything. Like I said, I try to just keep moving forward and focus on my work and my team. \n\nI guess... I mean, maybe there's a tiny, fleeting thought sometimes if I see someone drop something heavy, but it's not like a constant worry or anything that bothers me. I just brush it off. I really try to stay positive and not let things get to me. I hope that's the kind of attitude you're looking for? I just don't want to seem like I'm constantly complaining or worrying over nothing, you know?\n\nDoctor: Outside of the office, do you generally feel like you're more on edge or alert than most people, even in calm or safe environments?\n\nPatient: Um, outside the office? No, definitely not. I'm completely relaxed when I'm not at work. But... I guess, since you're asking about being on edge... when I'm actually *in* the office now, I have noticed I do this little thing where I keep checking where my boss is, or, um, where the exits are. And I kind of keep looking to see whether anyone is coming up behind me. \n\nIt happens even when I'm supposed to be focused on my team. I mean, it's not a huge deal, I just try to brush it off as being extra aware of my surroundings, you know? I really hope that doesn't make me sound paranoid or like I'm slacking as a supervisor, because I really am trying my best to do a good job and be there for my guys. Is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice yourself being more alert or easily startled in places outside of work, like at home or in public spaces, even when there’s no reason to feel unsafe?\n\nPatient: Um, no, definitely not. Like I said, outside of the office, I'm totally fine. I mean, at home or out in public, I'm just my normal self. I don't feel jumpy or like I'm looking over my shoulder at all. \n\nI guess I just leave all that nervous energy at work, you know? Once I'm out of there, I can just relax and be a regular guy. I really try to keep work stress at work, and I think I do a pretty good job of it. I mean, it's important to have that balance, right? I hope that's a good sign that I'm handling things okay... I really just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling more tense, restless, or 'keyed up' even when there's no specific reason or trigger for it?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't think I just sit around feeling tense for no reason. Like I said, outside of work I feel pretty relaxed, and even at work, I'm just trying to stay focused on my team. \n\nI guess sometimes I might feel a little restless if I'm just sitting at my desk too long, but I think that's pretty normal, right? Just needing to stretch my legs or whatever. I don't feel like I'm constantly keyed up or anything like that. I really try to keep a level head. I hope that makes sense? I just don't want to make it sound like I'm a nervous wreck when I'm really trying my best to keep things under control.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other physical symptoms lately, like muscle tension, trouble sleeping, or feeling irritable, even if it's not tied to a specific situation?\n\nPatient: Um, physical symptoms? No, I don't think so. I mean, my sleep is fine, honestly. I usually just crash when I get home because, you know, work takes a lot out of you. And irritable? Not at all. I really pride myself on being easygoing, especially with my team. I think a good supervisor has to be patient, right? \n\nI guess maybe my shoulders get a little stiff sometimes, but that's just from hunching over a computer or, you know, regular office stuff. It's nothing out of the ordinary. I definitely wouldn't say I'm walking around tense or snapping at people. I try really hard to be a positive presence for the guys. That's the right way to handle it, don't you think? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea—I'm really doing okay overall.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the office, do you find that your reactions to things like loud noises or sudden movements feel more intense compared to how you react to similar things outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to make a big deal out of it, because offices are naturally kind of noisy, right? But... yeah, I suppose that's actually the main reason I ended up calling the Employee Assistance Program. \n\nIt's just... lately, every little bang in the office—like, if someone slams a file drawer shut too hard, or drops a coffee mug in the breakroom—I just jump really hard. Like, way more than I should. And then my chest just keeps racing for a long time afterwards. It's silly, I know. I really try to play it off so the guys don't notice, because a supervisor shouldn't be jumping at shadows, right? I just... I want to be strong for my team. Is it normal to get a little jumpy like that when you're under stress? I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting by bringing it up.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the office, do you feel like you're constantly on guard or scanning your surroundings more than usual, even when there's no immediate threat or reason to be?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm *constantly* on guard. That sounds a little extreme, you know? But... I guess I have been a little more aware of things lately. \n\nIt's just, when I'm at my desk, I find myself kind of... checking where my boss is, or making sure I know where the doors are. And if someone walks up behind me, I kind of need to know they're there. I mean, I'm supposed to be focused on my team and helping them out, but I just keep glancing around. I try not to make it obvious, because I don't want the guys thinking I'm distracted or, you know, paranoid or something. It's probably just me being a little too cautious, right? I mean, it's good to be aware of your surroundings, isn't it? I just hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself reacting strongly—like jumping or feeling your heart race—to sudden or unexpected things, like a phone ringing or a door slamming, when you're not at work, such as when you're at home or in other everyday places?\n\nPatient: Um, outside of work? No, definitely not. When I'm at home, I'm actually really calm. Like, if my phone rings or my roommate drops something in the kitchen, I don't even flinch. \n\nI really try to just... leave the work stuff at the office, you know? I think it's super important to have that boundary, so I make a point to just unwind and be my normal self when I'm off the clock. So yeah, home is totally fine. That's a good sign, isn't it? Like, it means I'm still managing things pretty well overall, right?\n\nDoctor: When you're in everyday places, like walking down the street or sitting in a café, do you ever find yourself noticing every little sound or movement around you more than most people would?\n\nPatient: Um, no, definitely not. When I'm out getting coffee or just walking down the street, I'm totally fine. I don't really notice every little thing... I'm just kind of minding my own business, you know? \n\nI think I'm actually pretty good at just tuning things out when I'm not at the office. I mean, I want to be a well-rounded guy, not someone who's always on edge everywhere they go. I like to just relax and enjoy my time off. So, yeah, no issues like that outside of work at all. That's a positive sign, right? I try really hard to keep a positive attitude for the team. Being approachable is super important to me, so I definitely don't let little things get to me. I think I handle the day-to-day stress pretty well, don't you think? It's just normal work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these reactions to loud noises in the office are significantly affecting your ability to do your job or interact with your team in any way?\n\nPatient: Um, well... And, um, I've had to ask my team to double-check a few things that I'd usually just handle on my own. But I'm still showing up and trying hard, so that's the main thing, right? I know it sounds kind of silly to come in just for that, but it was starting to distract me from my team, so I figured I should ask a professional. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm handling it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced or witnessed a traumatic event, such as an accident, assault, or something life-threatening, that might be connected to your current reactions?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, definitely nothing like a major car crash or an assault or anything like that. I know that's usually what causes people to get jumpy, right? \n\nMy life is pretty normal, honestly. I haven't been in any life-threatening situations... at least, nothing that I think would really count. Is it possible to just get like this from regular, everyday stress? I hope I'm answering your question right, I just... I can't really think of anything huge that would cause this. Am I making sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden episodes of intense fear or discomfort that came out of nowhere and reached a peak within minutes, like a panic attack?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. It's really just... just that racing chest when there's a loud noise, like I mentioned before. \n\nI definitely don't want to make it sound like my situation is worse than it actually is.
